# 1796.

<summary>This is the title page of a 1796 occult manual that presents itself as a "Telescope" or "Key" to the secret divinatory practices of the ancient Magi. It identifies its subject as the "Great Kabbalah," a system for uncovering hidden knowledge through mystical interpretation.</summary>
<keywords>Zoroaster, Kabbalah, Divination, Magi, Esotericism, 1796, Occult philosophy</keywords>

# PRIVILEGED PERSON

### Placed at the highest rank in the social order.
<note>The term "Privileged Person" (French: *Privilégié*) refers to an individual belonging to the upper estates, such as the nobility or high clergy, who held legal and fiscal advantages before the revolutionary era.</note>

->MY LORD,<-

WHILE this sad end of the century <note>Likely referring to the turmoil of the late 1700s, specifically the era of the French Revolution.</note> sees the majority of your **PEERS** swept into the Labyrinth of a disastrous politics, deafened by the roar of war, and exhausted by the task—ever urgent—of keeping the infernal plague of revolutions far from their respective *orbits*, **YOUR LORDSHIP** alone has set the astonishing example of a philosophical economy of time. By this means, despite a thousand cares and a thousand dangers, You have known how to secure for yourself all the leisure and all the serenity required by the admirable ambition of a mortal eager to rise above objects that are not merely earthly.

From that happy age, My Lord, when you did not yet wear the magnificent chain <note>This likely refers to a formal "chain of office" or a decorative collar worn by high-ranking nobles or officials to denote their rank and service to the crown.</note> which honors you less than you honor it yourself; when beckoned by the enchanting voice of pleasures, you breathed the incense of a thousand tributes, and the

perfume, even more exquisite, of a thousand delicate delights, you dared to direct piercing gazes toward the height of several mysterious points, inaccessible even to any faint desire for contemplation on the part of the common herd of men.

This glorious impulse, **My Lord**, deserved, it is said, to be rewarded by certain **Beings** foreign to our coarse elements; but who willingly manifest themselves here below when they are suitably invited by *Humans* worthy of obtaining the distinguished favor of such an interaction. <note>The original term "commerce" implies a spiritual or social exchange/communion between the human and divine realms.</note> If it is true, **My Lord**, that you have enjoyed this, then for **You**, the existence of certain creatures superior to **Man**—with whom he is permitted, at times, to enter into relationship—is no longer a question for debate.

**Your Lordship**, consequently, knows what to think regarding the contemptuous incredulity of a multitude of *Sophists*, <note>In this 18th-century context, "Sophists" refers to Enlightenment philosophers whom the author views as using clever but fallacious arguments to promote atheism or materialism.</note> hatched from the license and immorality of the current age, who, for the most part, hardly admitting that a **Supreme Being** influences us, exhaust themselves in false reasoning and sarcasm, with the primary goal of erasing, if it were possible, even the slightest trace of a belief (*superstitious, they say*) in ***Superhuman Beings*** *communicating more or less perceptibly with man.*

In vain, a thousand striking examples, appearing one after another, attest that certain **Causes** (occult to the multitude) have the right to influence either a general period of time (affecting an entire country or a whole people) or the entire course of an individual life, or various of its eras; these occult **Causes**, or their **Agents**, subject...

<summary>The author praises the recipient for their spiritual courage in seeking hidden truths and suggests they have achieved direct contact with supernatural beings. He defends this mystical worldview against the "sophists" and skeptics of the Enlightenment who dismiss such beliefs as mere superstition.</summary>

<keywords>mysticism, spiritualism, Enlightenment, Superhuman Beings, occultism, divine providence, skepticism, Sophists</keywords>

...often MAN’S *imagination* undergoes combinations that are involuntary on his part, which provide him with warnings; which inspire in him insurmountable feelings of *sympathy* or *aversion*; which make him experience *presentiments*, and stir him with fears or hopes that time never fails to realize. <note>The author is arguing that "imagination" is not just fantasy, but a faculty that receives external spiritual signals.</note> None of this disconcerts our so-called <term>Freethinkers</term>: <note>Original: "ESPRITS-FORTS." A term used in the 18th century for "strong-minded" skeptics who rejected religious and supernatural explanations in favor of reason.</note> they remain stubbornly enclosed within the narrow circle of a system of *materialism* and *compression toward the earth*; <note>Original: "compression vers la terre." This refers to a philosophical refusal to look beyond the physical world to the spiritual realm.</note> a blind stubbornness through which these <term>Leaders</term> <note>Original: "CORYPHÉES." Originally the leaders of a Greek chorus, here used to describe the prominent figures of the Enlightenment movement.</note> of the so-called *philosophy* of the present time show themselves to be quite analogous to the owl, formerly given as an accessory to the image of WISDOM.... Was it intended, then, by means of this dismal symbol, to indicate to future generations that, in all times, following a Deity whose mission can only be luminous, there trailed—as if to defy her—a procession of bizarre mediators, enemies of the light and hiding from it, while the Eagle (to whom decorum forbids me from comparing anyone here) seeks and withstands the most brilliant radiance of the star of day. <note>The "star of day" refers to the sun, a common symbol for divine truth.</note>

When, through the melee of a thousand so-called *philosophical* opinions, though more or less erroneous, it becomes necessary to launch the <term>Elementary Principle</term> <note>Original: "RUDIMENT." Here meaning the foundational teachings of a deeper, spiritual truth.</note> of a doctrine of eternal truth: when, in spite of those so-called *Freethinkers* whom I have already defined, I propose to make a ray of vivid light penetrate the orbit where these birds of night close their eyes with affectation, for fear of being forced to admit that it is possible to *see* what they assert, on the contrary, does not exist; when the result of my work is to refute what these faithless skeptics...

<summary>The author criticizes Enlightenment "freethinkers" for their stubborn materialism, comparing them to owls who hide from the light of spiritual truth. He positions his work as an "elementary principle" of eternal truth intended to challenge their skepticism.</summary>
<keywords>imagination, freethinkers, materialism, philosophy, spiritualism, Enlightenment skepticism, symbolism of the owl and eagle</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's introductory defense of his work, likely following a dedication or an opening statement about the philosophical climate of the time.</meta>

...proclaim the duty of a subordination (the only reasonable one, they say) of all our moral & physical impressions to the testimony of our material organs, guided by that instinct expressed by the (hardly significant) phrase **COMMON SENSE**, as if the palpable and the visible were the *ultimate peak* <note>original: "NEC PLUS ULTRA" — the highest point or final limit</note> of divine creative omnipotence, while all of that still falls far short of the boundaries of our faculty of conception; in a word, when it comes to publishing the ABCs of the ancient system of **ZOROASTER** <note>Referring to the legendary Persian prophet, often invoked in Western occultism as a source of primeval wisdom</note> regarding what may exist this side of the **CREATOR** & beyond **US**; Lord! It is undoubtedly with You alone that a work of this kind must seek a shelter worthy of the sublimity of the subject, and which can be respected by the fierce detractors that the **TELESCOPE OF ZOROASTER** will inevitably encounter.

The doctrine treated in this work is extra-material, it is true, but without deception; it is bold, but without irreverence toward the **ETERNAL**. — Its goal? — It is innocent, it is religious. In the final analysis, this fragment of the ancient **GREAT CABALA** <term>Great Cabala: Here referring to a general system of hidden or esoteric spiritual knowledge, rather than exclusively the Jewish Kabbalah</term> teaches **MAN** that nothing happens to him here below that is not either some benefit from his **CREATOR**, or some punishment inflicted by the paternal hand of this merciful **BEING**, who is equally severe in His justice. Furthermore, the **TELESCOPE OF ZOROASTER** is addressed only to the very small number of Humans who are destined not to remain completely ignorant of the most beautiful privilege that **MAN** can enjoy. One will learn there that it happens, and how, that this **KING** of earthly creatures maintains honorable and no less fruit- <note>The text cuts off mid-word; likely "fruitful" or "fructueux."</note>

<summary>In this preface, the author defends his esoteric work against rationalist critics who rely solely on sensory "common sense," arguing that his "Telescope of Zoroaster" reveals higher spiritual truths within the framework of divine justice.</summary>
<keywords>Zoroaster, Esotericism, Common Sense, Great Cabala, Divine Justice, Enlightenment Critique, Spirituality</keywords>

== But what! (the vehement enemies of this **SUBLIME**—which they derisively label as the **MARVELOUS** <note>In the 18th century, "the marvelous" (*le merveilleux*) was a technical term for supernatural elements in literature, often used by Enlightenment skeptics to dismiss spiritual claims as mere fantasy.</note>—will cry out here with one voice.) Even if one were to grant that the hypothesis of certain perfected creatures communicating with man were admissible, would it be reasonable to apply the belief in *superhuman* **BEINGS** to the **DIVINATORY ART** (if indeed that itself can be admitted)? Yet a divination through them is precisely what this bold writing—which hastens to take refuge under the aegis of a Great Man!—treats exclusively! Even if one were to grant (these same arguers continue) that it is not always pure **CHANCE** that causes certain facts—which over-excited brains might have imagined in advance—to be realized, shouldn't this alleged divination at least manifest itself in the most distant future? And yet, is it not this very divination that one immediately affects to place before the eyes of the *uninitiated* <note>original: *l'impérit*, referring to someone lacking skill or the specialized knowledge of the craft.</note>, to whom the *cabalistic A-B-C* should seemingly only teach the first steps in such a vast career? == These objections, far from being final, are quite easy to refute. Here is the response:

Curiosity, in order to become completely active, needs to create a seductive goal for itself: the more sublime it is, the more it is excited; the more difficulties it presents to overcome, the more it becomes ca-

...capable of perseverance and effort. What would it matter to most individuals here below, even to those who are most susceptible to being permeated by some great idea, *whether there existed* or did not exist superhuman INTELLIGENCES *appointed by the* MOST HIGH *to maintain certain relations with* MAN, if the latter had only a sterile interest in seeking their commerce! In this commerce, would not the role of man remain absolutely passive, if communication depended solely on the whim of these PERFECTED SPIRITS, whose very mission does not allow them to manifest themselves indiscriminately to all classes of the human species! It is therefore not enough for a man animated by an interest more pressing and more noble than that of a vain curiosity to conceive of the existence of superhuman INTELLIGENCES; or to be convinced that one can *provoke* <note>original: *provoquer*. In this context, it refers to the act of summoning or inducing a response from spiritual beings through specific ritual or intellectual means.</note> them, or to know the method for doing so; it is far more necessary that the human BEING fortunate enough to be destined for the favor of communicating with *superhumans* should discover, from his very start in the cabalistic career, the full immensity of the expanse that the *immaterial guides* are soon to enable him to traverse.

It is thus that, on all the moral paths here below, the man of genius disdains to submit himself to gradations; he occupies himself solely with reaching the goal; he flies toward it in a straight line: he seizes it.... The CALLED <note>original: *l'appellé*. This refers to an individual who feels a spiritual "calling" or vocation to the occult sciences.</note> who is capable of becoming an enlightened *Cabalist* <note>A practitioner of the Cabala, a mystical and esoteric system of interpreting scripture and the universe.</note>, burns with an even more impetuous enthusiasm; the VOICE which speaks within him has warned him in advance not to suffer any kind of hindrance: from the very opening of the *Rudiment* <note>original: *Rudiment*. This refers to the elementary principles or the introductory "A-B-C" of the cabalistic system mentioned on the previous page.</note>, he has conceived...

<meta>This page continues from page viij, completing the thought regarding the "Called" individual who is capable of grasping the entirety of the cabalistic science.</meta>

->( ix )<-

...the entirety of a science.... whose most sublime end is to allow the **Cabalist** <note>In this context, a "Cabalist" refers to a practitioner of Western Esotericism or Hermeticism who uses mystical calculations and spiritual communication to uncover hidden truths.</note> to pierce through the darkness of time.

However, on the **Candidate’s** path, many an attribute, satisfying without being so distinguished, offers itself to encourage his zeal and reward him for his painful labors; but these detailed matters would be truly useless here: they would even cause confusion in a concise work where **Your Lordship** will remark that it is essentially only a question of a *mechanical* <note>original: *méchanique*. This refers to the specific ritualistic or mathematical operations used in the art, which the author distinguishes from the necessary spiritual inspiration.</note> process, the rest remaining implied in the *deduction*, which follows, *of the qualities* presupposed to be vested in the future **Cabalist**, and in the announcement of a denial of success, made at the same time to anyone who has not been favored in advance by that fortunate *inspiration* which I have designated everywhere as being the effect of the breath of the **Pure Spirit**.

Now, the knowledge of Time is the only fruit that we may gather from an intimate commerce with superhuman **Intelligences**. And this, because Time, the *fabric of life*, is the sole property of us material **Beings** destined to end. Time is the unique foundation upon which that which is important for us to know can be traced for our benefit. This knowledge alone is within our reach, alone for our use, while awaiting the enjoyment of that opposite of Time, of which we can only form an imperfect idea under the name of *Eternity*.

**Divination** is an extension of sight, either into a Past that would not have been transmitted to us by any tradition, nor by any testimony of our...

<summary>The author explains that the primary goal of the Cabalist is to master "Time" through spiritual communication, though success requires a divine "breath of the Pure Spirit" beyond mere mechanical ritual. He defines divination as a way to perceive a past or future that is otherwise inaccessible to finite, material beings.</summary>

sense, recorded in the register of our memory; whether in a *Present* that would be beyond the reach of our physical organs; or finally in the *Future*.

The invaluable *privilege* of this *extension of sight* has nothing *miraculous* about it; it is merely *superhuman*. The gift is conferred upon us by the celestial INTELLIGENCES <note>In occult philosophy, "Intelligences" refer to spiritual beings, such as angels or planetary spirits, who mediate between the Divine and humanity.</note>; it is through their care that this *gift* develops within us, more or less easily, sooner or later, according to the degree of favor that it pleases the MOST HIGH to bestow upon us. The seed of the PROPHET can reveal itself suddenly in a child; the development of this seed is sometimes late, or limited even in the most diligent of Devotees <note>original: "Avoués". While this usually means legal advocates, here it refers to those "avowed" or dedicated to the study of these mysteries.</note>. Divination is therefore not a GOAL, placed at the same distance for everyone, and which one must certainly reach like a *Doctorate* in some purely human science. But like these sciences, the divinatory cabalistic ART <term>cabalistic: referring here to the "Kabbalah of the Magi," a tradition of mystical interpretation used to uncover hidden truths about the universe and the future.</term> involves a *nomenclature* and a certain process, designed to consolidate the foundations of ideas and facilitate their classification. Providing this nomenclature and teaching this process is the sole purpose of our KEY to the great Kabbalah of the Magi. By it, the precept will become common to all; but a certain CALLED individual may find themselves so richly endowed with inspiration that they would already be too delayed by the mere nine STEPS that the TELESCOPE OF ZOROASTRE <note>A reference to a specific system of divination or a 1765 book of the same name which used numbers and tables to predict the future.</note> teaches; another, on the contrary, will accomplish them with difficulty; more than one presumptuous CANDIDATE will not even finish the short task of this career’s beginning: this *disavowed Aspirant* will soon recognize that curiosity is not a vocation, and that, unfortunately, he is made

<summary>This preface explains that divination is a superhuman gift bestowed by celestial beings rather than a skill that can be mastered by everyone like a standard academic degree. It introduces the author's work as a "Key" to the Kabbalah of the Magi, intended to provide a structured method for those with the inherent potential for prophecy.</summary>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the spiritual "extension of sight" and the role of celestial Intelligences in granting access to the hidden mysteries of the Kabbalah, specifically addressing why some are excluded from these revelations.</meta>

page xj

...so that, for him, the Kabbalah remains forever silent. This is because the superhuman Intelligences themselves must open a certain barrier, which will remain closed to the *disavowed* <note>original: "désavoué". This refers to those who are not spiritually "acknowledged" or accepted by the celestial powers.</note>, while once this passage is crossed, no obstacle appears across the immense space that separates the most recent of the Admitted from the one who has approached closest to the throne of the Eternal.

„ But! (one might still object) who can guarantee to the *uninitiated* Mortal that these celestial gatekeepers will favor him? — No one will guarantee it to him.

I ask in turn: what is the necessity for an individual who does not believe in *divination* <term>divination: here, the mystical art of perceiving hidden truths or future events through spiritual aid.</term> (a sure sign that he will not even grasp what he apparently supposes to be merely an ingenious fiction)—where, I say, is the necessity for such a Skeptic to occupy himself with the Great Kabbalah, and to conditionally desire to enter, through it, into a relationship with the celestial Intelligences—that is to say, in the case where perhaps some wonder, comparable to that by which Paul was converted, would finally demonstrate to him that their existence is no fable? <note>A reference to the biblical account of the Apostle Paul, whose sudden and miraculous conversion followed a divine vision on the road to Damascus.</note>

Feeling compelled to believe in these superhuman Beings, being internally moved by them—that is the only proof one can have that they exist. Where will one find any champion of *materialism* <term>materialism: the philosophical belief that nothing exists except matter and its movements, denying the existence of the soul or spirit.</term> firm enough to step into the arena, armed with a stronger proof that they do *not* exist? — "One cannot believe in them," (he will say) "*because... I cannot conceive of them!*" — But, you automaton! (one replies to him) do you then claim that by the short reach of your strictly earthly ideas, one must measure all

<summary>The author argues that spiritual revelation requires inner belief and the favor of celestial beings, rather than logical proof. He dismisses skeptics and materialists who demand miracles before they will believe, asserting that the impulse to believe is the only evidence required.</summary>
<keywords>Great Kabbalah, Celestial Intelligences, skepticism, materialism, spiritual initiation, St. Paul, divination</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the rhetorical challenge against the skeptic or materialist who doubts the existence of spiritual beings. It follows the argument that internal feeling is a form of proof and questions the arrogance of limiting reality to what can be perceived by the physical senses.</meta>

page xij

...the possible extent of other men's conception? Are you then so sure of exercising a sound judgment, even regarding things most immediately subject to the reports of your material organs, that it would be sensible on your part to declare *impossible to conceive* and (by an even falser consequence) *non-existent*, that which is entirely foreign to you, and which escapes beyond your reach to immeasurable distances? And if it is a matter of choosing between two premises equally inaccessible to verification—the existence or the non-existence *of a middle hierarchy between heaven and earth* <term>middle hierarchy: a traditional mystical concept referring to a chain of being, such as angels or spirits, that exists between the physical world of humans and the divine realm.</term>—do you mean to contest my right to lean in favor of existence? This belief warms me; it flatters me with the hope of a communion through which I may sometimes find myself released from the yoke of MATTER, while *non-existence* chills me and binds me more closely to the condition of the ANIMAL. The latter is perhaps happier for having only its brute instinct as a guide, which leads it consistently well enough; whereas MAN, alone gifted here below with a REASON, a GIFT OF THOUGHT (which would end with him, just as it begins with him, if there existed no superhuman creatures) <note>The author suggests that human thought is not an isolated phenomenon but the bottom rung of a ladder of higher intelligences; without those higher rungs, the "gift of thought" would have no context or destination.</note>—whereas, I say, man, in spite of his fine privilege, is the plaything of a thousand errors from which the ANIMAL at least is exempt.

If this reasoning is just, is not the pride of MAN absurd, when he refuses to think that Heaven may have destined for him guides more certain than what are called INSTINCT, COMMON SENSE, REASON? Is this pride not ungrateful if, conceiving the idea of these superhuman guides, man disdains to implore their salutary assistance?

<summary>The author argues that human reason is fallible and that it is more logical and comforting to believe in a hierarchy of superhuman guides than to limit reality to material existence. He suggests that without these spiritual beings, the human gift of thought would be an evolutionary dead end compared to the reliable instinct of animals.</summary>
<keywords>middle hierarchy, reason vs instinct, spiritualism, materialism, human condition, celestial guides, gift of thought</keywords>

->( xiij )<-

But, everything of which **MAN** was one day to be certain to the point of evidence, was previously denied by these same **False-Sages** <note>original: *Faux-Sages*; a term used to describe self-proclaimed philosophers or materialists who reject spiritual truths</note> who, at all times, have refused to believe in what they could not conceive. The first among the Geometers who suspected that there were *Antipodes* <term>Antipodes: places on the opposite side of the earth where people would stand "foot-to-foot" with those on the other side</term>, did not fail to find opposed to his idea all those so-called *friends of truth* who yield to nothing for which their physical organs have not yet given them full certainty. However, people traveled; the spherical shape of the earth was recognized; it was finally known, to the point that one could no longer pretend to doubt it, that men existed at the point situated diametrically below the one where stubborn reasoners were still striving to deny that the other hemisphere was habitable.

In truth, one will never be able to make discoveries as demonstrative in the *intellectual regions*; but what we call **Fable** (which is but a *History of ancient truth*, diluted in an allegorical jumble, of which, unfortunately, the original sense is lost to us), proven **History**, the *oral tradition of all ages*; the *sacred books* for the use of all religions; do not all these authorities vie with one another to proclaim that there exist **superhuman Beings**, *below the* **Creator**, *above the* **Creature** <note>In this context, "Creature" refers to created beings, specifically humanity, as a class of existence</note>, *sometimes interpreters to the latter of the favor of the* **Almighty**, *at other times (but always more rarely)* **effective Ministers** *of his* **wrath!**

However, the pride of ignorance, and especially that (much more stubborn) of *false knowledge*, opposed—just as they will continue to oppose from now on—the taking root of any great truth whatsoever.

<summary>The author compares the historical denial of the Earth's sphericity and the existence of the Antipodes to the contemporary denial of spiritual hierarchies. He argues that ancient fables, histories, and sacred texts all point to the existence of intermediary superhuman beings between God and humanity.</summary>

<keywords>skepticism, materialism, Antipodes, allegory, spiritual hierarchy, superhuman beings, False-Sages, ancient truth, providence</keywords>

They will fight it with more passion, as it shows itself to be more sublime; the conviction to which this truth compels is too humiliating for those who do not wish to admit they are struck by it. Nothing has been able to *define* these relationships nor prove them. But, starting from the hypothesis that it was possible for **GOD** to permit them, nothing can demonstrate either that believing in them is a chimera.

On this basis, the **CABALA** indicates a formula (which is traced back at least to Zoroaster <note>original: *Zoroaſtre*; the ancient Persian prophet traditionally viewed by Western occultists as the founder of magic and the first of the "Magi"</note>) by means of which it is in the power of the **PRIVILEGED** person either to seek out *super-human* **INTELLIGENCES** when he needs to consult them, or to easily understand their language, when they *themselves* have the kindness to anticipate the wish of the confidence placed in *them*.

# EPILOGUE OF THE EDITORS.

While this work was being printed, the Editor <note>original: "Rédacteur"; referring here to the author or compiler of the occult system.</note>, apparently desiring that a greater number of Enthusiasts <note>original: "Amateurs"; in the 18th-century sense of dedicated seekers or practitioners of an art.</note> might benefit from it, kindly sent us models of hexagons that are more distinct than those used by experienced Cabalists <note>Practitioners of the Western Hermetic Kabbalah, a mystical tradition involving planetary and angelic symbolism.</note>, and which constitute the hexagons described on Page seven, following the epistolary dissertation.

The difficulty of executing in inlay and on wood everything we found added (in the new hexagons), whether to the *Figure* or to the *Number* that each of them expresses, led us to decide to have this tool <note>original: "ustensile"; here referring to the physical tiles or components used to operate the "Telescope."</note> manufactured more suitably, with an impression taken, for each piece, from a Plate similar to the large-format one seen at the end of this copy: in this way, one will have gathered on each piece a *Figure*, or a *Number*, and the *Planetary Sign*, along with the *Zodiacal Sign* that each of these pieces entails: plus, the name of its *Intelligence* <note>In Renaissance and Enlightenment occultism, an "Intelligence" is a presiding spirit or consciousness governing a specific planet or celestial sphere.</note>, or its *Angel*.

But the Editor, in making such an expansion favorable to our private interest, has requested in return that we announce that it is with some regret that he entered into this detail, which is of a nature to make the Candidate negligent; whereas the latter should, above all, apply himself to becoming, through practice, unperturbably familiar with...

->*<-

<summary>The publishers explain that the author provided more detailed designs for the divinatory hexagons, which now include planetary, zodiacal, and angelic names, though the author fears these additions might make students lazy.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the specific instructions regarding the "Sum" angel and temporal regimes into an editorial disclaimer regarding the transcription's accuracy and a peculiar recurring error in the astrological symbolism used by the original author.</meta>

( 6 )

We have observed, for our part, that the editor, whether by design or through inadvertence, has everywhere employed the sign ♏ <note>The astrological symbol for Scorpio.</note> for the virgin <note>Virgo</note>, and the sign ♍ <note>The astrological symbol for Virgo.</note> for the scorpion: this is a transposition contrary to standard usage. Furthermore, this quirk, or this error, is of no consequence once one has been notified of it.

The same cannot be said for several inaccuracies which the very nature of the work itself did not allow us to avoid, especially as we were printing far from the supervision of the manuscript’s author. These inaccuracies are rectified by the *errata* <note>A list of corrections for errors found in the printing; from the Latin for "errors."</note>; we request that the reader consult it as often as the text appears to be ambiguous <note>original: "équivoque"</note> to them.

( xviij )

It was not with impunity either that, even earlier, the same **CABALIST** <note>In this context, a "Cabalist" refers to a practitioner of the Cabala (Kabbalah), a system of Jewish mysticism that was adapted by European occultists into "Cabalism" to predict the future through numbers and letters.</note> had cast some rays of light; both in a frightful whirlwind, where the association of crimes of every kind prepared the earth for the overflow of various plagues that one sees desolating it today; and in another whirlwind, wrongheadedly virtuous, where madmen, harmful in other ways, imagined nothing better than to fill a useless abyss with the corpses of an entire generation of **Brothers** <note>original: "Freres." This likely refers to members of the Freemasons or other initiatic secret societies, many of whom were executed or persecuted during the French Revolution.</note>—an abyss that need not have been crossed before infallible collapses, which will naturally be produced by some new shocks, had partly closed the monstrous chasm on their own....

Wherever human madness causes some public calamity, the oppressors are irritated by the setback that a flash of light brings to the execution of their criminal projects; the oppressed, in their turn, are offended to find themselves suspected of having become—only through a lack of common sense—dupes and finally victims. However, in such cases, a deplorable evidence has well demonstrated that the one who anticipated the future had guessed too correctly; but folly persists in attributing to **CHANCE** <note>original: "HAZARD"</note> alone that which is incontestably consistent between the predictions and the disastrous events that justified them.

But let us return to what more particularly concerns the **GREAT CABALA** as a **METHOD** and vehicle for reaching a result of *divination*.

( xix )

...a place of *meeting*, where the PETITIONER <term>Avoué: literally an "attorney" or "advocate," but here used to describe the initiate who presents themselves before higher powers</term> may enjoy the favor of being in the presence of *superhuman* BEINGS and can receive their blessings there.

Such an order of things once existed wherever the Magi lived—those revered Priests, the most enlightened and best of MORTALS. In their religious palaces, consecrated to the PURE SPIRIT, these sacred servants, inaccessible to the curiosity of the vulgar <note>original: "vulgaire." In this period, this refers to the common people or the uninitiated, rather than a comment on their character.</note>, devoted themselves without distraction to the sublime communion that their mission was to maintain with the AGENTS OF HEAVEN. These agents dictated to the favored CASTE everything regarding the secrets of celestial and terrestrial NATURE that could be brought within reach of human understanding—which is always infinitely limited, no matter what degree of penetration one supposes in the most perfectly organized men endowed with the greatest genius.

With time, those *upheavals* to which the surface of our globe is subject; those *revolutions*, even more fatal still, occasioned (at ever-frequent intervals) by the extravagance and ferocity natural to the greater number of its so-called *rational* inhabitants; in a word, everything that can cooperate in destruction, ruined the temples of the MAGI, and even more unfortunately, dissolved their sublime federation. A treasure of the most vast knowledge—an immense product, continually increased by individual contributions poured into the common ark by each of the SEERS <term>Voyans: individuals gifted with the "second sight" or the ability to perceive spiritual realities</term>, who had succeeded one another without ceasing for an infinity of centuries—this mysterious treasure was finally swallowed up. Barbarians, almost immediately, took possession of it.

Unworthy of having this all-powerful intervention continue for them, a *parasitic sect* composed of the *superstitious* and *deceivers* <note>original: "jongleurs." Literally "jugglers," but here referring to those who use sleight-of-hand or trickery to feign mystical powers.</note> soon succeeded in altering everything that had fallen into their hands. Gradually, it lost even the last vestige of the ancient and pure process, just as it had lost sight of the holiness of the goal of the *Cabalistic science* <term>science cabalistique: here referring to a Christianized form of theurgy or mystical study aimed at divine communication</term>. From then on, there arose that MAGIC called *black* or *diabolical*, that so-called ART which (because it has always turned a crowd of contemptible frauds into the playthings of the *chastising* INTELLIGENCES) can also pass, in the eyes of the ignorant masses, for something superhuman. This is an error, however, whose worldly depravity and utter worthlessness beyond the boundaries separating the *terrestrial* and the *celestial* were never misunderstood by the truly wise.

By these fools, by these guilty deceivers, everything was thrown into confusion. The GOOD-PRINCIPLE and the EVIL-PRINCIPLE <note>A reference to dualistic concepts where light and darkness are viewed as competing powers.</note>, having become rivals in the opinion of these UNGODLY men, shared equally in their profane incense. Cain was even more devoutly sought after than Abel <note>The author uses the biblical brothers Cain and Abel to represent two spiritual paths: Abel represents the path of divine favor, while Cain represents the path of earthly toil and, in this context, the origin of forbidden or "black" arts.</note>: according to the doctrine of these madmen, the self-styled **MAGICIANS**, they henceforth expected from the grace of **DEMONS** what they had finally despaired of obtaining from that of the **ANGELS**. But these all-too-lasting abuses could change nothing regarding the *immutable truth of the relationships which, by the permission of the Almighty, exist between earth & heaven.* In the moral realm <note>original: "Au moral." In this period, "the moral" refers to the spiritual, psychological, or metaphysical world, as opposed to "the physical" or material world.</note>, no more than in the physical, that which is an element can neither be denatured nor perish. All the wealth that was accumulated by the ancient order of the **MAGI**, therefore still exists: it can thus, and must, be found again without loss and in all its purity, for us and our descendants. At whatever depth it may be buried, it is for us to dig and find it again. Superstition alone will be able to oppose this excavation: it is in this sense that, spiritually speaking, it is very true that a Demon stands sentinel *in every place that conceals a treasure.*

No matter: it is noble to undertake that which must have a glorious success. No, without doubt: rather, we must presume that this *inspiration is an order emanating* from the celestial **INTELLIGENCES**, and a pledge of the certain protection they grant to intentions as pure as the object of these labors is sublime.

<margin>4</margin>
# KEY

<margin>4</margin>
### OF THE

<margin>12</margin>
# GREAT KABBALAH
<note>The term "Kabbalah" (from the Hebrew *Qabbalah*, meaning "tradition" or "received knowledge") refers here to a system of mystical and esoteric philosophy used to interpret the universe.</note>

<margin>8</margin>
## OF THE MAGI.
<note>Magi (singular: Magus) refers to masters of the occult sciences and ancient wisdom.</note>

---

### INTRODUCTION.

Before learning to read, one has learned to know the Alphabet, to combine its letters into syllables and into words: soon after, a meaning, a phrase, and finally an entire discourse are organized in the understanding of the Reader: subsequently, he may compose for himself.

Before being a Musician, one has learned to know the notes, their value, their position, the meters, and the slow, moderate, or brisk movements that these meters indicate. Soon the physical and moral *rhythm* <note>original: "rithme"</note> of Music makes itself felt. One hears the language that melody and harmony speak together: with invention and sensitivity, one can become a composer oneself.

<summary>This introductory page presents the "Great Kabbalah" as a structured discipline, comparing the mastery of mystical secrets to the foundational steps of learning to read or play music. It suggests that once the basic "alphabet" of the craft is understood, the practitioner can move from understanding to creation.</summary>
<keywords>Kabbalah, Magi, occult education, music analogy, literacy, mysticism, initiation</keywords>

--- Page 52 ---
<meta>Continuing from the introduction, this page transitions from the general analogies of literacy and music to the specific "science" of the Great Kabbalah, emphasizing its role in predicting the future and the need for divine inspiration.</meta>

In the same way, before taking the first step in the career of a science infinitely more intellectual than eloquence and music, one must learn the language of the vast country through which one will travel. THE GREAT KABBALAH, which we are about to treat, is this *science*: the country where one intends to travel is THE FUTURE.

The GREAT KABBALAH has, in order to be understood, the equivalent of an *alphabet* that is exclusively its own. From the combination of what here takes the place of *letters*, there also result *words*, *periods* <note>In a rhetorical sense, a "period" refers to a complete, well-structured sentence or thought.</note>, *discourses*, and finally extensive, precise *Fragments*, which by proceeding *well*, one can snatch from the immeasurable HISTORY OF THE FUTURE.

At first, one will find it quite easy to read and trace our cabalistic characters: but that will not yet mean understanding the language; understanding it will not yet mean possessing it. After having *read* much and *written* much, one might remain for a long time still, or perhaps forever, very ignorant in the matter of the GREAT KABBALAH.

The PURE SPIRIT must have breathed upon the Candidate. This person, from then on, no longer writes anything that does not soon offer a *meaning* without a shadow of ambiguity. This Privileged one, *maturing* imperceptibly, in proportion to the care they take with *prudence* and *without prejudice* <note>original: "sans prévention" — meaning without preconceived notions or bias</note>, can yield, in the long run, the precious (but very rare) fruit of a...

<term>Great Kabbalah: the study of mystical traditions used here as a tool for divination.</term>
<term>History of the Future: the concept that the future is a recorded narrative that can be "read" through esoteric means.</term>
<term>Pure Spirit: a divine or supernatural influence necessary for true understanding of the occult.</term>

<summary>The author explains that the Great Kabbalah possesses its own "alphabet" and language used to uncover the "History of the Future." He warns that technical mastery of these symbols is insufficient without the inspiration of the "Pure Spirit" and a mind free of prejudice.</summary>

## FIRST STEP.

IT leads to the knowledge of the material utensils <note>Original: "ustencile." The author uses this term to describe the physical tools—the papers and tokens—used to perform the calculations of the Kabbalah.</note> necessary for the mechanical operations involved in the **GREAT KABBALAH**, and to knowing how to put a portion of these utensils to use.

### UTENSILS.

Three squares of paper (we shall state in due time and place what they are to be inscribed with) and a certain number of small pieces (of wood, cardboard, or even simple playing cards) cut into perfectly regular hexagons with sharp angles: such is the only equipment indispensably necessary for the material process of consulting the enormous **BOOK OF DESTINY**.

Having announced that we would defer speaking of the three papers, we move on to the definition of the **HEXAGONS**.

<sig>A 3.</sig>

<summary>This page introduces the "First Step" of the Great Kabbalah, moving from theory to the practical tools required: three marked squares of paper and several hexagonal tokens. These physical items are described as the essential "utensils" for consulting the "Book of Destiny."</summary>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 5, which introduced the physical "utensils" (papers and hexagons) required for the mechanical operations of the Great Kabbalah.</meta>

( 6 )

->### HEXAGONS.<-

According to what experience has shown us, the greatest convenience requires: 1st. that these Hexagons be made of wood, preferably to any other material; 2nd. that, being nine lines <note>A "line" (*ligne*) is an archaic unit of measurement used before the metric system, roughly equivalent to 2.25 millimeters or 1/12th of a French inch.</note> wide at most, from one angle to the one diametrically opposite it, they should never have less thickness than three-quarters of a line; never more than a line and a half; 3rd. that the wood be somewhat firm, not brittle, and capable of receiving, within half of its thickness, an inlay <term>incrustation: a decorative technique where a piece of material is set into a carved-out space on the surface of another</term>; 4th. that this inlay be white, and made of wood, preferably to ivory.

The inlay (circular in shape and having its center at an equal distance from the six angles) is intended to receive some sort of impression in black. Good quality writing ink is sufficient for this purpose when the inlay is made of wood; the impression is then permanent; on ivory, it would easily rub off.

The invariable position of every *piece* such as we have just described, is to present an angle to the North; an angle, consequently, to the South, and a side terminated by two angles to the East, as much as to the West.

(*See Plate I, the position of the pieces.*)

<summary>This page details the physical specifications for the hexagonal tokens used in the Kabbalistic system, including their material (wood), size in "lines," and the necessity of a white circular inlay for permanent ink markings. It also establishes the mandatory orientation of these pieces relative to the cardinal directions.</summary>
<keywords>hexagons, wood, inlay, measurement, lines, ink, orientation, North, South, East, West</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical description of the "utensils" for the Great Kabbalah, moving from the physical construction of the wooden hexagons to their terminology, symbolic orientation, and the precise quantity required for the operations.</meta>

## T E R M S.

Before being used, the hexagon described is a *piece*, or *part*. When put to use, it is variously called a *place*, *square*, or *cell*. Its angles are also called *points*; and its flanks, *sides*, or *faces*.

For our purpose, **North** and **Zenith** are synonymous, as are **South** and **Nadir**. <note>The author aligns the physical directions of the board with cosmic coordinates; the Zenith is the highest point in the sky directly above an observer, and the Nadir is the point directly opposite it, below the earth.</note>

## Q U A N T I T Y.

One hundred and twelve of the described hexagons are indispensable, although no more than thirty-seven are ever used at once. It is advisable to have one or two dozen spares <note>original: "de relai"</note> without markings, both to be able to mix some insignificant ones into certain operations and to replace those that might break or be lost. As they must be absolutely identical, both on top and underneath, it is not a matter of indifference that they be remade as they go missing. It is difficult to match the shade and the grain of the wood; furthermore, wood darkens as it ages: substituted pieces, initially perfectly similar, would soon become darker, which would be a defect. One must never operate before having scrupulously verified the count of one hundred and twelve: if it were incomplete, the missing piece must be identified immediately and replaced.

<summary>This page establishes the specific terminology for the hexagonal pieces and their orientations, while emphasizing the necessity of maintaining a complete set of 112 identical wooden tokens to ensure the integrity of the Kabbalistic operations.</summary>
<keywords>terminology, Zenith, Nadir, wooden hexagons, set quantity, maintenance of instruments, ritual consistency</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues from the discussion of the physical properties and quantities of the wooden hexagons, moving into how these pieces are arranged and the symbolic terminology used to describe those arrangements.</meta>

( 8 )

# USE OF THE PIECES.

The described hexagons are used to create *combinations*, or a *marquetry* <note>meaning an inlaid pattern or mosaic of pieces</note>, in the form of *triangles*, *lozenges* <note>diamond shapes</note>, and *hexagons*.

These are called *figures* when one considers only their exterior, or *outline* <note>original: "trait moyen," likely referring to the boundary lines of the shapes</note>.

The interior, or *fullness* <note>original: "plein"</note>, gives these same *combinations* the quality of *tableaus* <note>meaning a decorative arrangement or picture</note>.

Finally, these combinations are *mirrors* when, instead of merely speaking to the eye as *tableaus*, they offer the CABALIST <note>a practitioner of Kabbalah, a school of thought concerned with mystical or esoteric interpretations</note> a *meaning* that can exist for him alone. It is because they *reflect the truth* with which these combinations are saturated that they begin to deserve the name of *mirrors*.

## RECAPITULATION.

Thus, by combining the hexagonal *pieces* or *parts* into composite *triangles*, *lozenges*, or *hexagons*, one obtains *tableaus* and *mirrors*.

Now, since the *mirror* is the final object of the *kabbalistic* process, it is the quality of the *mirror* that prevails, and this word is the one most commonly used.

---

<vocab>hexagons, marquetry, triangles, lozenges, kabbalist, mirrors, recapitulation</vocab>

<summary>This page defines the three levels of hexagonal arrangements: "figures" (outlines), "tableaus" (visual patterns), and "mirrors" (symbolic arrangements reflecting spiritual truth for the practitioner).</summary>
<keywords>marquetry, tableaus, mirrors, kabbalist, symbolic geometry, lozenges, hexagons</keywords>

( 9 )

## THE SECOND STEP.

It leads to an understanding of all the figures included in the GREAT CABALA <note>original: "GRANDE CABALE," referring to this specific system of mystical geometric arrangements</note>, and how to construct them.

### F I G U R E S.

The smallest combination (which consequently results in the smallest figure) is a triangle composed of three pieces.

The most extensive combination is a hexagon of thirty-seven pieces.

There can be no *triangles* nor *lozenges* <note>diamond-shaped figures</note> more extensive than those that can be contained, without exceeding the boundaries, within the surface of the largest hexagon just mentioned.

These limits allow for only four *triangles*, three *lozenges*, and three *hexagons*. (*See Plate 1.*) <note>a "Plate" refers to a full-page illustration or engraving found in the back of the book</note> (*)

---

(*) To avoid multiplying the number of figures, only one *large triangle* and one *large lozenge* have been drawn, each of which contains all the respective smaller figures. However, the formation and boundaries of these are indicated by numbers and letters. *A* begins each of the general figures. The smallest end at *B*; the larger ones then at *C*; even larger at *D*, etc. The formation of the hexagons is likewise indicated in Plate III with numbers and letters.

# T R I A N G L E S .

*First:* This is the one consisting of three pieces of which we have already spoken: it is called the *small triangle*. ( *See Plate I, a-b.* ) <note>A Plate refers to an illustrated diagram provided in the original manuscript.</note>

*Second:* It is composed of six pieces, and is called *simple* or *empty*, because it presents only lines at its center. ( *See Plate I, a-c.* )

*Third:* It is composed of ten pieces and is called *full* <note>original: "plein," meaning solid or filled-in</note>, because its center is a *part*, or piece, surrounded by nine others. ( *See Plate I, a-d.* )

*Fourth:* It is composed of fifteen pieces, and is called *double*, because its center is itself a small triangle. It is also called the *large triangle*, being in effect the largest ( *actual* ) triangle allowed by the cabalistic process. <note>The "cabalistic process" refers to the specific rules of this mystical system for arranging symbols.</note> ( *See Plate I, a-e.* )

---

# L O Z A N G E S . <note>These are diamond-shaped figures.</note>

*First:* It is composed of four pieces and is called *small*, being in effect the smallest practicable size, and *empty*, offering at its central point (that is, at the intersection of its perpendicular and horizontal lines) only a stroke forming part of the first line, and cut by the second at its midpoint. ( *See Plate I, a-b.* )

*Second:* It is composed of nine pieces, and—

## H E X A G O N S.

*First*: Consisting of seven pieces; it is called *small*, and *orbital*, insofar as it forms a figure similar to the one that, in the GREAT MIRROR <note>The "Great Mirror" likely refers to a primary master-diagram or a specific stage in this mystical process.</note>, forms an *orbit*; which will be explained in its turn. ( *See Plate III, from a to b.* )

*Second.* It is of nineteen pieces, and is called *medium*, insofar as it is neither the smallest nor the largest figure of its proportion; and *double*, insofar as it contains another composite hexagon. ( *See Plate III, a-c.* )

*Third.* It is of thirty-seven pieces and is called *large*, being not only the largest hexagon permitted, but also the largest of all the figures. It is also called *triple*, because it contains two concentric hexagons, not to mention the central piece. Finally, it is called *total*, because it contains all the figures allowed by the cabalistic process <note>original: "procédé cabalistique"; referring to the systematic rules for arranging these geometric symbols.</note>. ( *See Plate III, a-d.* )

### OBSERVATION.

Any isolated figure is called <term>real</term><note>French: "réelle". In this system, it refers to a complete, standalone diagram.</note>: it thus forms a *picture* or a *mirror*. Any figure that is *enclosed* or *contained* within another is called <term>ideal</term><note>French: "idéale". Likely referring to internal or conceptual structures that do not stand alone.</note>. An opportunity will arise to explain the purpose of this important distinction.

---

## THE THIRD STEP.

This step leads to knowing what the one hundred and twelve small <term>primitive hexagons</term><note>The basic six-sided building blocks used to construct the larger figures in this system.</note>—which serve to compose the ten known figures—contain in terms of *compartments* or *cells*.

### CONTENTS OF THE CELLS.

Each of the one hundred and twelve primitive pieces is the *compartment*, *place*, or *cell* for either a **Principle**, a **Spirit**, an **Intelligence**, or finally a **Number**, from *one* up to ninety-nine inclusive:

| Nine **Intelligences**, here . . . | 9 |
| Ninety-nine Numbers, | 99 |
| **Total . . . . . . . . . . .** | **112 pieces.** |

## P R I N C I P L E S .

Of the two Principles, one is infinitely good: it is SISAMORO; the other is infinitely evil: it is SENAMIRA. What seems to prove that our <term>Kabbalah</term><note>A mystical or esoteric system of interpretation; here used to refer to the secret tradition of this specific divinatory art.</note> indeed comes to us from the Persians is that SISAMORO is nothing more than the reverse of OROMASIS, and SENAMIRA the reverse of ARIMANES—both being so contradictorily powerful according to the religion of those ancient Peoples. <note>Original: "OROMASIS" and "ARIMANES." These are the Greek/Latin versions of Ahura Mazda (the supreme god of light) and Ahriman (the spirit of darkness) in Zoroastrianism. The author notes that "Sisamoro" and "Senamira" are anagrams or reversals of these names.</note> Are not all doubts regarding the origin and antiquity of our divinatory masterpiece removed by this respectful tradition, which transmits to us, under a thin veil, names so authentically indicative? This remains true even though many other denominations have since been applied to the same objects—which, for us Christians, correspond to GOD and the DEMON!

SISAMORO is represented, in his lodge, by a RADIANT DELTA. (See Plate II.) <note>The "Radiant Delta" is a triangle—often containing an eye or the name of God—surrounded by rays of light, a common symbol of the Divine in both Masonic and Christian iconography.</note>

SENAMIRA is represented, in his own, by a flaming MANED COMET, accompanied by thunderbolts and hail. (See Plate II.) <note>The term "maned comet" (French: *comète crinée*) refers to the traditional description of a comet's tail as a head of hair (Latin: *coma*).</note>

### General Qualities of the Intelligences.

GENHELIA <term>Genhelia; likely derived from the Greek roots for "birth" or "origin" and "sun," suggesting a life-giving solar force</term> presides over birth, growth, and the formation of organs; over health and all good natural affections; over family bonds, from fatherhood to the most distant affinity. This INTELLIGENCE is pure, sympathetic, serene, gentle, active, and readily favorable.

SELENO <term>Seleno; derived from the Greek "Selene," the goddess of the Moon</term> presides over the same matters as GENHELIA; but he is base, selfish, temperamental <note>The original term "humoriste" refers to the "four humors" or bodily fluids thought to control a person's mood and health.</note>, and lazy. He disputes with his rival over all bodily habits contracted through the influence of secondary causes <note>A philosophical term for the natural laws and earthly circumstances that carry out the will of a primary cause (God or Fate).</note>; over all inclinations that education can modify, and he willingly corrupts what GENHELIA strives, on the contrary, to improve.

EROSIA <term>Erosia; derived from Eros, the Greek god of love and desire</term> exclusively governs LOVE, whether as a passion of the soul or as the universal mode of reproduction. She knows all the delights and all the torments that love entails; all its pleasures and all its pains; all its moral excesses

**PANURGIO** <term>Panurgio; likely derived from the Greek "panourgos," meaning "ready to do anything," often associated with cleverness or roguery</term> presides over the agility of the body, over its feats of skill and strength, and over sharpness of wit. She prepares all great fortunes and unexpected distinctions. Opulence, victory, and triumph attach themselves wherever she casts a glance. She sublimes all good sentiments, exalts fidelity, constancy, friendship, love, and courage. She maintains the fire of genius: she pulls man from the worst straits, and purifies him to the point of making him reach the highest degree of perfection of which human nature is capable.

### S I N G U L A R I T I E S

By examining the Table of numbers (Plate II) with attention, one will observe:

1st. That the *primitive digit* is the only one found four times in its *own column*.

2nd. That the second time it appears, it is accompanied by a zero which *increases it tenfold*.

3rd. That each column offers a *doublet* <note>a number composed of two identical digits, such as 11, 22, or 33</note>.

4th. That the four numbers composed of two digits—which are neither the *doublet* nor the *number ending in zero*—are reversed within the *same column* <note>for example, if a column contains 12, it will also contain 21</note>.

5th. That no other number than the *primitive* appears there more than twice.

# THE GREAT CABALISTIC MIRROR.

As we recall, and Plate III reminds us, this is the hexagon of thirty-seven pieces, or triple? <note>The question mark appears in the original; it likely refers to a specific "triple" configuration of the 37 tokens used in this divinatory system.</note>

## O B S E R V A T I O N S.

*First.* Although all the pieces that compose the GREAT MIRROR have, according to the rule, a point directed toward the North, the arrangement nevertheless presents a face, just like all <term>composite hexagons|Geometric figures formed by smaller, individual hexagonal units</term>.

*Second.* The thirty-seven pieces that compose the GREAT MIRROR represent the quantity that most closely approaches one-third of 112 (the total number of pieces in the set), leaving behind only a single part, since three times 37 equals one hundred and eleven. **Now**, as the two <term>principles|In this system, these are likely two special foundational pieces that are set aside from the standard "mirrors" or layouts</term> never enter into the composition of any mirror, and since the probability—in the ratio of two to one—favors one of the two PRINCIPLES emerging among thirty-seven pieces, it follows that the most accurate third of the 112 pieces is *thirty-seven*, which is required for the formation of the GREAT MIRROR.

## A S P E C T S.

The GREAT MIRROR will be considered in turn—

turn under two very different aspects, or at least which have very little analogy between them. One is *active*, & is called *Sidereal* <note>meaning "relating to the stars"</note>, or *astronomical*. The other is *passive*, & is called *temporal*, or *chronic* <note>referring to time or chronology</note>. In speaking of one or the other of these aspects, it is called a <term>REGIME</term> <note>from the French "Régime," here meaning a system, rule, or mode of operation</note>.

### *Sidereal or Astronomical Regime.*

Under the aspect of, or according to the *astronomical regime*, one observes in the <term>GREAT MIRROR</term> <note>likely a symbolic diagram or a specialized tool used in this occult practice</note> a *center* & *three Zones*. The center is a single piece or part, (See Plate III.) & this part has no other name in the <term>GREAT MIRROR</term> than *center*, or *focus*.

## Z O N E S.

It is known that *Zone* means *belt*, or *surroundings*? Thus the three Zones offered by the <term>GREAT MIRROR</term> encircle its *center*, or *focus*, three times. These *Zones* are named *central* or *solar*; *sidereal* or *planetary*, & *zodiacal* or *frontier*.

### *Zone centrale.*

It is also called *solar*, because the *center* it borders is the ideal place of the Sun; (according to the axiom adopted for more than one occult science, *the Sun in the middle* <note>original: "Sol in medio"</note>.) This *zone* is composed

### Sidereal Zone.

This *zone* is the closest to the *center* after the *solar* zone. Its name, *Sidereal* <note>From the Latin "sidereus," meaning relating to the stars or constellations</note> or *planetary*, comes from the fact that each of its six angles is the ideal position for a planet. Furthermore, it is composed of twelve *parts*. It begins at No. 8 and ends at 19. (See Plate III. a. c.)

### Zodiacal Zone.

This Zone is the one furthest from the *center*. It is called *zodiacal* because twelve of its *spaces* <note>original: "cases," referring to the individual cells or compartments in the diagram</note> are the ideal positions for the twelve *signs* of the *Zodiac*; and its nickname, *frontier*, comes from the fact that it bounds the extent of the <term>GREAT MIRROR</term> on all sides. It is composed of 18 *parts*, of which all those on the sides (that is to say, those that are not at the angles, numbering twelve in total) are assigned to the twelve *signs*.

### O R B I T S .

The *center* or *focus* <note>original: "foyer," referring to the central point of activity or light</note> of the <term>GREAT MIRROR</term>, together with the *zone* that immediately surrounds this center, form an *orbit*. Likewise, as each angle of the *sidereal zone* can be regarded as a particular center and the seat of a planet, the six *parts* by which each of these ideal centers is immediately surrounded form, in their turn, an *Ideal Zone*. This zone, along with the planet itself, determines an *orbit* specific to that Planet.

## P L A N E T A R Y  T R I A N G L E.

The **GREAT MIRROR** contains six of these triangles. They are those formed by two neighboring Planets with the Sun. The one formed by *Saturn*, *Mars*, and the *Sun* is called *west-sub-horizontal* <note>original: *ouest-sous-horizontal*; referring to the area below the horizon line on the western side of the Mirror.</note>. — The one for which *Mars* and *Venus* form the horizontal base is called *southern*. — The one for which *Venus* and *Jupiter* form the base is called *east-sub-horizontal*. — The one for which *Jupiter* and *Mercury* form the base is called *east-supra-horizontal* <note>original: *est-sus horizontal*; the area above the horizon line on the eastern side.</note>. — The one for which *Mercury* and the *Moon* form the base is called *northern*. — The one for which the *Moon* and *Saturn* form the base is called *west-supra-horizontal*.

## Z O D I A C A L  T R I A N G L E.

The same proportions; but the base is further from the *center*, and formed by two outer angular cells. Each base contains two signs of the Zodiac. (*Plate III provides an account of all this.*) They use the same names as the sidereal or planetary triangles; that is to say—

<summary>This page defines the six Planetary Triangles and the Zodiacal Triangles within the Great Mirror, naming them according to their compass directions and their position relative to the horizon line.</summary>

( 35 )

<sig>C 2</sig>

—to say that the triangle formed by cells 37, 22, and 1 of the **Great Mirror** is *west-sub-horizontal* <note>original: *ouest-sous-horizontal*; referring to the region below the horizon line on the western side.</note>, like its included one <note>Referring to the smaller planetary triangle nested within this larger zodiacal one.</note>, formed by *Saturn*, *Mars*, and the *Sun*. The same applies to the five other *triangles* whose base is in the *frontier zone* <note>original: *Zone frontière*; the outer region of the grid.</note> of the Great Mirror.

## EXTERNAL TRIANGLE.

This one, even more abstract <note>original: *idéal*; in this context, it refers to conceptual or geometric constructs rather than physical or manifest ones.</note> than those of the two classes defined, starts from one of the angles of the Great Mirror—either above or below the horizon—directing its base line perpendicularly or vertically toward the angle that is not the nearest: and, by two sides, ends at one of the two horizontal angular points. The Great Mirror contains only two triangles of this kind: namely, the one formed together by cells 22, 34, and 28 (it is indicated on **Plate III** by the capital letters **A**, **B**, **C**) and the one formed by cells 25, 31, and 37 (it is indicated on the same Plate by the capital letters **D**, **E**, **F**). These triangles have not been dotted <note>Likely referring to the use of dotted lines in the diagrams to distinguish overlapping shapes.</note> for fear of confusion. Nor have the small triangles that can be practiced in the *central zone* <note>original: *zone centrale*</note> been specified, following the example of those in the other two *zones*: a *Key* <note>A set of rules or a cipher used to decode occult meanings.</note> is not a *Treatise* <note>An exhaustive academic or philosophical work.</note>, and the Candidate to whom everything must be told would be ill-suited to knowing anything once they had been told everything.

<summary>This page concludes the description of the Zodiacal Triangles and defines the External Triangles (A-B-C and D-E-F), while emphasizing that the reader (the "Candidate") must use analogy and intuition rather than relying on an exhaustive explanation.</summary>
<keywords>Great Mirror, west-sub-horizontal, frontier zone, External Triangle, Plate III, central zone, occult geometry, candidate</keywords>

( 36 )

The observations to be made based on the *triangles*—whether those already described or those formed arbitrarily within the **GREAT MIRROR**—will be infinite. The care required to consult them will take time and some effort; however, this burden will grow lighter and eventually become almost nothing as these calculations become more familiar. In return, they will increase the variety, the richness, and especially the infallibility of what the results reveal a hundredfold.

## EASE OF OPERATION <note>original: *AISANCE*</note>.

The Candidate <note>The student or practitioner initiated into this system.</note> whose eye is not yet geometrically trained would do well, while operating, to always have a compass <note>A mathematical drawing tool (pair of compasses) used here to measure equal distances between points on the grid.</note> in hand. This will allow them to find, without error or difficulty, the third point required to complete a *triangle* with two numbers already chosen in the **GREAT MIRROR**. For example: if, from the center of cell 18, one has placed the other point of the compass on the center of cell 31; by fixing this second point there, the first point (once lifted) can only precisely find the center of cell 12. This is the third point completing an *equilateral triangle* <note>A triangle where all three sides are of equal length.</note> with 18 and 31—the only triangular figure to which the **GREAT CABALA** <note>The specific mystical or divinatory system detailed in this manuscript.</note> attaches any property. The same applies to all triangles one might imagine and whose formation is possible within the space of the **GREAT MIRROR**.

<summary>This page explains that while geometric calculations in the Great Mirror require initial effort, they ensure the accuracy of the results. It provides a practical method using a compass to identify equilateral triangles, which are the only triangles recognized as significant in this system.</summary>

## FIFTH STEP.

The previous step must have made the topography of the **GREAT MIRROR** very familiar to the Candidate. This step will lead him to more interesting and less dry objects. However, a prejudice might arise here and cause difficulty: it is a matter of destroying it.

->*Distinction and Connection.*<-

If the Candidate has some notion of either *divinatory astrology* <note>The practice of predicting earthly events or personality traits based on the positions of celestial bodies.</note> or *purely physical astronomy* <note>The scientific study of celestial objects and the physics of the universe, which began to separate strictly from astrology during the Enlightenment.</note>, he will first be struck by seeing that the *great Mirror* does not borrow its organization (nor everything that composes it—its essence) from any particular system, whether of ancient *astrology* or modern *astronomy*. We would observe to him: 1st. That the *great Cabala* <note>Refers here to the specific mystical or esoteric system detailed in this work, rather than just the traditional Jewish Kabbalah.</note> greatly surpasses in antiquity the oldest of these sciences, which were often broken by the revolutions of Nature and humanity; 2nd. That modern *astronomy* follows a different direction than the *great Cabala*, since this astronomy seems to disdain the *mystical* part of the *science of HEAVEN*: a part totally useless, indeed, to common men, while on the contrary, *astronomy*

## SIGNS OF THE ZODIAC.

The same lack of conformity with astronomical systems is felt in the **GREAT CABALA** regarding the signs of the Zodiac. They are found there, in truth, distributed in the order usually cited and expressed by this well-known distich <note>A distich is a pair of lines of verse that typically make a complete statement.</note>:

->♈ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♉ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♊ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♋ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♌ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♍<-
> *They are the ram, the bull, the twins, the crab, the lion, the virgin;*
> *the scales, the scorpion, the archer, the goat, the water-bearer, the fish.*
> <note>Original Latin: "Sunt aries, taurus, gemini, cancer, leo, virgo; libraque, scorpius, arcitenens, caper, amphora, pisces." This is a classic mnemonic rhyme used for centuries to remember the order of the zodiacal constellations.</note>
->♎ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♏ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♐ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♑ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♒ &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ♓<-

However, the **GREAT CABALA** deviates slightly from the order to which these signs are subjected in relation to the terrestrial seasons. The deeply philosophical spirit of a work that is claimed to go back as far as **Zoroaster** <note>The ancient Persian prophet and founder of Zoroastrianism, who in Western occult tradition was often regarded as the inventor of magic and astrology.</note>, and to be but an *essence* of the wisdom of this divine *Legislator*—his Cabala, let us say—had to subordinate the signs of the Zodiac instead to the different stages of human life. — On this basis, according to the distribution we are about to make known, the objective of the **GREAT CABALA** is perfectly fulfilled.

### Concerning the Life of Man, According to the Great Cabala.

It is well known that the rustic year (like the ancient Roman year) begins with the *Ram* <note>The sign of Aries.</note>,
which also opens our astronomical spring?
**In the same way, the Great Cabala** subordinates the 
beginning of man’s existence to the *Ram*; but
it does not place the *Ram*, as our calendars do, in the
spring-like and lively orbit of *Mars*.
( *Cast a glance at Plate III.* ) It
pushes this sign back into the wintry <note>Original: "hyémal," a term derived from Latin referring to the winter season.</note> and fune-
real orbit of *Saturn*, specifically into the last cell 
possessed by this planet ( *the 20th* ) in the zo-
diacal zone and immediately following the seat of
death ( *cell 37* ). This emblem, as philoso-
phical as it is true, serves as a reminder that the moment 
life begins, it touches upon nothingness, and that the 
human creature, who already breathes, yet enjoys none 
of its physical faculties or any of its moral 
privileges, must, for some time, re-
main suspended between life and death, re-
maining in the uncertainty of its preservation, and 
held fast in the bonds of weakness. **Saturn** 
is still there to devour his children <note>In Roman mythology, Saturn (Cronus) devoured his children at birth to prevent them from overthrowing him. Here, the author uses the myth to symbolize how fragile an infant's life is and how close birth sits to the "funereal" influence of time and death.</note>.

<summary>This page explains why the Great Cabala places the beginning of human life (Aries) within the orbit of Saturn rather than Mars, symbolizing the extreme fragility of the newborn.</summary>

### *Division of the Life of Man.*

<margin>60 X</margin>
Let us now imagine, beginning from the twentieth cell of the **GREAT MIRROR**, that all the cells composing the zodiacal zone are each equivalent to a **lustre** <note>A "lustre" is a traditional term for a period of five years.</note>, or five years. One must then remember that, according to the **Great Cabala**: 1st. *The total span of life is divided into six periods, each consisting of 15 years or three lustres.* 2nd. *Each corner of the Great Mirror closes one of these periods.*

On this basis, the total span of life (calculated as a favorable proportional average) would be 90 years, or 18 lustres. No doubt, in the ancient times when the *Great Cabala* was born, man—being of a better stock and more economical with his faculties—commonly reached an age that far exceeds the current duration of our life; today (as a moderate proportional average), we can hardly reckon life beyond fifteen lustres, or 75 years.

#### *First Period.*

We have seen man born in the 20th cell (that of the *Ram* ♈ <note>Aries</note>), adjacent to the vortex of Death (cell 37). Let us now see him, leaving the cold and gloomy orbit of Saturn, pass, at the end of his first lustre, through the...

->Second period.<-

But, beneath a path strewn with flowers, **SENAMIRA** (\*) relentlessly undermines him—*like a roaring lion, prowling about seeking someone to devour.* <note>original Latin: "tanquam leo rugiens, circuitu quærens quem devoret." ### *Third Period.*

Here begins the age of energy and virtues. There they acquire their full development. They are so well characterized by the **Lion** (Leo ♌) and the **Virgin** (Virgo ♍)! **The Being** must then find themselves skilled in everything that can finish perfecting them. Having reached the 28th cell, they have undoubtedly become all they could have aspired to be, or at least everything is well prepared for the infallible fulfillment of their reasonable wishes. They are at the summit of the mountain: they enjoy the richest era of their existence, if they have not previously abused it. — There ends the third period, that of **Maturity**.

# Fourth period.

Woe to the **BEING** if they have not yet digested their knowledge, consolidated their fortune, and cemented the reputation to which they should have aspired. Woe, in a word, if they have not sufficiently amassed; they will remain poor in everything they neglected to acquire. If they are not worthy of holding the **SCALES** (Libra ♎) where their experience should enable them to appreciate everything, it is they themselves who will be weighed; and if they are found wanting in weight, or if they are of base metal <note>original: "mauvais alloi." This term refers to coins or metals that are debased or of poor quality.</note>, public blame will punish them for the poor use they made of their best years. They will then have only those perilous resources that intrigue and illicit speculation wrest from foolishness; for, upon leaving the pure and luminous domain of **AGLAIA** <note>original: "Aglaé." In this system, Aglaia (traditionally one of the three Graces) is associated with the planet Jupiter (♃), representing benevolence and expansion.</note> (♃), they have fallen into the mists and the labyrinth of **PANURGE** <note>Panurge is a famous character from Rabelais's stories, known for his wit but also his cunning and lack of morals. Here, he is linked to the planet Mercury (☿) to represent trickery and the complexities of the mind.</note> (☿). There, anyone who did not bring with them the lantern and the thread of Wisdom <note>An allusion to Ariadne’s thread and Diogenes’s lantern, symbolizing the tools needed to navigate a moral labyrinth.</note> easily goes astray. There are found creeping agitation, shame, and remorse, so well represented by the **Scorpion** (Scorpio ♏), which stings and poisons itself. There, in short, are prepared all the punishments deserved by the cheat and the disrupter of social order. Such is, in the insidious orbit of **PANURGE**, the lot of the scoundrel, while

### *Sixth & last period.*

The last door has closed upon the being, like all those he has already passed. Cast upon a dark, sterile, and icy road, he can still drag himself along for a long time, struggling constantly against the infirmities expressed by the frosts of AQUARIUS (♒).
